### Notes

[^fn-p0294-1]: (1) As Sumu-abu founded the dynasty it is possible that the usual formula was not employed for his first year.
[^fn-p0294-2]: (2) The word <span class="small-caps">bad</span> (Semitic *dūru*), literally “wall,” means also “fortress,” and it is sometimes difficult to decide in which sense it is used. Whichever translation is employed, however, the meaning is not essentially altered, as the fortifications of ancient Babylonian cities consisted of huge walls of earth faced with sun-dried bricks.
[^fn-p0294-3]: (3) The first sign in the line is <span class="small-caps">aga</span>, not <span class="small-caps">bad</span> as in l. 34. If this rendering of the line be correct, the city of Kiš must have been allied with, or subject to, Babylon at this time. This was not the case during the reign of Sumula-ilu, in whose thirteenth year Kiš was captured and destroyed.
[^fn-p0296-1]: (4) Nearly fifteen hundred years before the time of Sumu-abu, the city of Kaṣallu was in conflict with the Semitic population of Babylonia. At that time Kaštubila, of the city of Kaṣallu, revolted against Sargon of Agade, but the latter marched against the rebels and defeated them. The “Omens of Sargon” relate that “he overthrew their mighty host, and he turned Kaṣallu into mounds of dust, and he left not so much thereof that the birds of heaven could roost upon it.” See *Cun. Inscr. West. Asia*, vol. iv, pl. 34, No. 1, Obv., ll. 31 f.
[^fn-p0296-2]: (5) The tablet Bu. 91–5–9, 475, is dated <span class="small-caps">MU I-ZI</span> *Su-mu-a-bu-um* <span class="small-caps">BA-TIL</span>, “The year in which the wall of Sumu-abu was destroyed”; this year is probably not to be referred to the reign of Sumu-abu, but to some subsequent period.
[^fn-p0296-3]: (6) The List of Kings assigns fifteen years to the reign of Sumu-abu.
[^fn-p0296-4]: (7) It is probable that the “Sumula-ilu-canal,” mentioned under the 12th and 32nd years of this reign, is the same canal as that termed the Šamaš-ḫegallu-canal in this line. If this be so, it may be inferred that the canal was repaired, or perhaps extended, in the 12th and 32nd years.
[^fn-p0297-1]: (8) A very similar formula was employed for the 28th year of Ḫammurabi.
[^fn-p0298-1]: (9) The capture of the city of Kiš was evidently regarded as a most important event, inasmuch as it gave the official title to five years of Sumula-ilu’s reign.
[^fn-p0298-2]: (10) The Babylonians named this year “the fourth year” of the taking of Kiš, as they included in their reckoning the year in which the city was taken.
[^fn-p0298-3]: (11) This line probably refers to a revolt of the city of Kaṣallu, which had been captured in the 13th year of Sumu-abu ; the formula implies that Iaḫar-zīr-ili, the leader of the revolt, went forth from the city and took the offensive against Babylon. Two years later (see the 20th year) the revolt was crushed by the destruction of the wall of Kaṣallu and the massacre of the inhabitants. Iaḫar-zīr-ili must have made his escape from the city, for it was not till five years later (see the 25th year) that the Babylonians succeeded in defeating the remainder of his forces and in slaying him.
[^fn-p0299-1]: (12) Fortresses, or walls erected for purposes of defence, were frequently dedicated to a god ; cf. the six *durāni*, or “fortresses,” which Sumula-ilu founded and which Samsu-iluna repaired (see above, pp. 201 ff.).
[^fn-p0300-1]: (13) These two signs are probably not to be taken as part of the ideogram for *Kutū*, the city of Cuthah, as the determinative <span class="small-caps">ki</span> is absent.
[^fn-p0300-2]: (14) From inscriptions of Samsu-iluna we know that a fortress, or wall, was built by Sumula-ilu in the city of Nippur, and named by him *Dūr-Zakar* after this god. In the course of time it fell into decay, but was restored by Samsu-iluna, who dedicated it to the goddess Nin-ḫar-sag (see above, p. 201).
[^fn-p0301-1]: (15) It is improbable that B<span class="small-caps">ar-zi(ki)</span> is an abbreviation for *Barzipa*, Borsippa.
[^fn-p0302-1]: (16) In the List of Kings the length of Sumula-ilu’s reign is given as 35 years. The tablet Bu. 91–5–9, 2,184, is of interest, for it proves that during the reign of Sumula-ilu a vassal-king named Bungunila held sway over some portion of Babylonia; the tablet is dated <span class="small-caps">mu gar bu-un-gu</span>(?)-<span class="small-caps">un-i-la lugal-e</span>, and the names of Sumula-ilu and Bungunila are associated in the oath-formula. This tablet also proves that the date-formulæ given in the Chronicle were not universally employed throughout the country, and in support of this fact the dates upon the contracts, Brit. Mus., No. 92,636 (Bu. 91–5–9, 2,172) and Bu. 91–5–9, 318, may be cited; both these tablets belong to the reign of Sumula-ilu, but their date-formulae do not correspond to any in the Chronicle.
[^fn-p0302-2]: (17) This year is cited under a Semitic formula upon a tablet in Berlin (V.A.Th., 706, see Meissner, *Altbabylonisches Privatrecht*, No. 79) as follows : *šattu Za-bu-um a-na bīt a-bi-šu i-ru-bu*, “The year in which Zabum entered into his father’s house,” i.e. ascended the throne.
[^fn-p0302-3]: (18) 19 The restoration of this line is taken from the date upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,524, which reads : <span class="small-caps">mu uš</span>(<span class="small-caps">-sa</span>) *Za-bi-um* <span class="small-caps">lugal-e</span>.
[^fn-p0303-1]: (19) The formula for this year occurs in an abbreviated form upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,480, which is dated <span class="small-caps">MU E-SAG-IL</span>.
[^fn-p0303-2]: (20) The tablet Bu. 91–5–9, 2,189, is dated in this year ; the formula there reads <span class="small-caps">MU ALAM</span> *Za-bi-um* <span class="small-caps">BA-DIM-MA</span>, “The year in which the image of Zabum was made.”
[^fn-p0303-3]: (21) The reading of the name Kaṣallu is quite clear upon the tablet. This date, in an abbreviated form, is found upon the tablet Bu. 91–5–9, 2,463, where the year is cited as <span class="small-caps">MU BAD</span> *Ka-ṣal-lu*(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>). The restoration of the end of the line as <span class="small-caps">BA-GUL</span> is uncertain, the verb upon the “case” of Bu. 91–5–9, 2,463, beginning <span class="small-caps">NE-</span>[ . . . ]. From the spacing of the signs which are preserved, it would seem that some such additional phrase is missing as is given under the 21st year of Sumula-ilu.
[^fn-p0304-1]: (22) This date occurs in an abbreviated form upon the tablet Bu. 88–5–12, 43, where it is given as <span class="small-caps">MU E A-AB-BA ḪE-GAL</span>.
[^fn-p0304-2]: (23) This estimate of the length of Zabum’s reign agrees with that given in the List of Kings.
[^fn-p0304-3]: (24) Upon Brit. Mus., No. 92,660 (Bu. 91–5–9, 2,498), this year is cited under the following Semitic formula: *šattu A-pil-Sin a-na bi-it a-bi-šu i-ru-bu*, “The year in which Apil-Sin entered into his father’s house”; cf. p. 220, note 17.
[^fn-p0304-4]: (25) Partly restored from the date upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,421, which reads <span class="small-caps">MU BAD MAḪ GI KA-DINGIR-RA(KI)</span> *A-pil*-*<sup>ilu</sup>* *Sin* <span class="small-caps">BA-RU</span>, “The year in which Apil-Sin built anew the great wall of Babylon.”
[^fn-p0304-5]: (26) The formula for the 5th, 11th, or 14th year should perhaps be restored from the date upon Brit. Mus., No. 92,583 (Bu. 88–5–12, 711), which reads: <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *A-pil*-*<sup>ilu</sup>* *Sin* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E BAD</span> *Du-ur-mu-ti*(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) <span class="small-caps">BA-RU</span>, “The year in which Apil-Sin the king built the fortress of Dūr-mūti.” It is also possible that to one of these three years (or to the 6th year) the building, or repair, of the temple of Ištar of Babylon should be assigned. This event is referred to under a Semitic formula in the date upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,477, which reads : *šattu šubat <sup>ilu</sup>Ištar* [*ša B*]*ābili<sup>KI</sup>* [*A-pil*]*-Sin i-pu-šu*, “The year in which Apil-Sin built the dwelling of Ištar of Babylon ”; cf. the date upon Bu. 88–5–12, 38.
[^fn-p0305-1]: (27) Possibly read <span class="small-caps">mu uš-sa</span> . . . . , “The year after that in which . . . .”
[^fn-p0305-2]: (28) Restored from Bu. 91–5–9, 586, which gives the date in the abbreviated form <span class="small-caps">mu</span> (<span class="small-caps">id</span>)*A-pil-Sin-*<span class="small-caps">ḫe-gal</span>.
[^fn-p0306-1]: (29) It is possible that this year is referred to under an alternative formula upon Bu. 88–5–12, 265, which is dated <span class="small-caps">MU UŠ-SA</span> *ša-ka-ra-am a-na* *<sup>ilu</sup>* *Šamaš A-bi-il-Sin rē’ū* (?), “The year after that in which Apil-Sin, the Shepherd, (poured out) the drink-offering (?) to Šamaš.” If this be the case, the alternative formula refers to the religious ceremonies which accompanied the inauguration of the throne of the Sun-god, and we may perhaps assign to the 17th year the tablet Bu. 91–5–9, 2,484, which is dated *šattu A-pil-Sin u-ra-am-mi-ku*, “The year in which Apil-Sin poured out (the libation).”
[^fn-p0307-1]: (30) This estimate of the length of Apil-Sin’s reign agrees with that given in the List of Kings.
[^fn-p0307-2]: (31) The reading *Lib-ba-tum* is possible.
[^fn-p0308-1]: (32) The formula for this year is abbreviated on contracts to <span class="small-caps">mu bad</span> (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)Z<span class="small-caps">a-kar-da-da</span>, see Bu. 88–5–12, 256, and Bu. 91–5–9, 280. The Berlin tablet V.A.Th. 733 is also to be assigned to this year; see the traces of the date given by Meissner, *Altbabylonisches Privatrecht*, pl. 48, No. 101.
[^fn-p0308-2]: (33) Brit. Mus., No. 92,655 (Bu. 91–5–9, 2,455), is dated in this year; the formula is there abbreviated to <span class="small-caps">mu</span> (<span class="small-caps">id</span>) (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)A<span class="small-caps">i-ḫe-gal</span>.
[^fn-p0308-3]: (34) To this year the tablet Brit. Mus., No. 92,654 (Bu. 91–5–9, 2,451) is to be assigned; the date-formula there employed agrees with that given in the text.
[^fn-p0308-4]: (35) This date is found in the abbreviated form <span class="small-caps">mu bad</span> M<span class="small-caps">uru</span>(<span class="small-caps">ki</span>) upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,504.
[^fn-p0308-5]: (36) Upon contract-tablets the formula for this year is generally abbreviated to <span class="small-caps">mu</span> (<span class="small-caps">id</span>) T<span class="small-caps">u-tu-ḫe-gal</span>: see Bu. 88–5–12, 31, 46, 60, 341, 404, 721; Bu. 91–5–9, 1,021; V.A.Th. 782 (Meissner, No: 17), etc.
[^fn-p0309-1]: (37) The tablet Bu. 88–5–12, 285, is dated in this year under the abbreviated formula <span class="small-caps">MU BAD EREŠ(KI)</span>.
[^fn-p0309-2]: (38) It is probable that Brit. Mus., No. 92,637 (Bu. 91–5–9, 2,173), should be assigned to this year ; it is dated <span class="small-caps">MU GIŠ-GU-[ZA] (DINGIR)AL-MU (?)</span>, but the name of the god is not clearly written. The Berlin tablet V.A.Th. 905 (Meissner, No. 88) is dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> <span class="small-caps">GU-ZA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">URU-KI</span>, and, according to Meissner’s copy of the reverse, this tablet should be assigned to the reign of Sin-muballiṭ (cf. the oath-formula in l. 12 f.). If this were the case, it would be possible to restore the name of the god in the formula for the 16th year of Sin-muballiṭ as (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">URU-KI</span>, i.e. Nannar. But the 3rd year of Ḥammurabi takes its title from the making of a throne for Nannar, and it is unlikely that the same date-formula would have been used twice within a period of eight years. It is therefore preferable to suppose that the traces of the royal name in V.A.Th. 905, l. 13, should be read [ . . . *-a*]*m-mu-*[ . . . ], i.e. [*Ḥa-a*]*m-mu-*[*rabi*], in place of Meissner’s reading *Sin-mu-*[ . . . ], i.e. *Sin-mu-*[*ba-li-iṭ*].
[^fn-p0310-1]: (39) This line is restored from Bu. 88–5–12, 290, which is dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> <span class="small-caps">I-SI-IN</span>(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) <span class="small-caps">IN-DIB-</span>[<span class="small-caps">BA</span>]. In the abbreviated form <span class="small-caps">MU</span> <span class="small-caps">I-SI-IN-NA</span>(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) the date is found upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,466 ; and the name of Isin, with the first syllable written as <span class="small-caps">I</span> instead of <span class="small-caps">NI</span>, occurs in the date upon Bu. 91–5–9, 447, which reads [ . . . . ] <span class="small-caps">I-SI-NA</span>(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) <span class="small-caps">IN-DIB</span>. The capture of the city of Isin in this year formed an epoch for dating tablets in some parts of Babylonia ; this is proved by the dates upon many of the contracts of Rim-Sin which were found at Tell Ṣifr. Thus, Brit. Mus., No. 33,204 (B. 47), is dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> <span class="small-caps">UŠ-SA</span> <span class="small-caps">V-KAM</span> <span class="small-caps">I-SI-IN-</span>[<span class="small-caps">NA</span>(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>)] <span class="small-caps">BA-AN-DIB</span>, “The fifth year of the taking of Isin”; Brit. Mus., No. 33,195 (B. 38), is dated in the sixth year of the same epoch, No. 33,207 (B. 50) in the seventh year, No. 33,163 in the eighth year, No. 33,280 (B. 123) in the ninth year, No. 33,197 (B. 40) in the thirteenth year, No. 33,194 (B. 37) in the eighteenth year, and No. 33,202 (B. 45) in the twenty-eighth year. A tablet at Constantinople, in the Imperial Ottoman Museum, is even dated in the thirtieth year of the taking of Isin (see Scheil, *Recueil de travaux*, xxi, 1899, p. 125) ; this year would correspond to the 26th year of Ḫammurabi.
[^fn-p0311-1]: (40) The formula for this year has been partially restored from the dates upon Bu. 88–5–12, 157, and Bu. 91–5–9, 332, which read : <span class="small-caps">MU</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)Utu (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)Mer.
[^fn-p0311-2]: (41) This line should possibly be restored from Bu. 91–5–9, 2,181, which is dated <span class="small-caps">MU UMMAN ARARMA(KI)</span>, “The year in which the people of Larsam ( . . . . )” ; it is also possible that the tablet should be assigned to the fourteenth year, when the inhabitants of Larsam may have shared the fate of the people of Ur.
[^fn-p0311-3]: (42) In their estimates of the length of Sin-muballiṭ’s reign, there is a considerable discrepancy between this chronicle and the List of Kings ; the latter assigns thirty years to Sin-muballiṭ.
[^fn-p0311-4]: (43) The accession-year of Ḫammurabi is referred to in the dates upon Bu. 91–5–9, 1,058 (<span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E</span>), and Bu. 91–5–9, 2,467 (<span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL</span>) ; the still shorter form <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* occurs upon Bu. 88–5–12, 110, and Bu. 91–5–9, 2,502.
[^fn-p0311-5]: (44) This date is cited on Bu. 88–5–12, 33, in the abbreviated form
[^fn-p0312-1]: (45) To judge from the spacing of the signs and from the similar formulæ for the 12th, 14th, and 16th years, it may be concluded that the verb <span class="small-caps">MU-UN-NA-DIM-MA</span> was omitted at the end of the line. This date in the abbreviated form <span class="small-caps">MU GU-ZA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">URU-KI</span> occurs upon Bu. 88–5–12, 220, 744 (Brit. Mus., No. 92,594), and V.A.Th. 905 (see above, p. 227 f., n. 38). On V.A.Th. 755 (Meissner, No. 16) occurs the longer formula <span class="small-caps">MU</span> (<span class="small-caps">GIŠ</span>)<span class="small-caps">GU-ZA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">URU-KI KA-DINGIR-RA</span>, “The year in which the throne of Nannar (was made) in Babylon.”
[^fn-p0312-2]: (46) In addition to the destruction of Malgā, Ḫammurabi also destroyed the fortress of Maer in this year of his reign, and the two events are sometimes coupled together in the dates upon tablets inscribed during the year. Thus, Bu. 91–5–9, 44, is dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E INIM</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">ANA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">EN-LIL-TA BAD MA-ER</span>(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) <span class="small-caps">U MA-AL-GE-A</span>(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) <span class="small-caps">MU-UN-GUL</span>, “The year of Ḫammurabi the king in which at the word of Anu and Bēl the wall(s) of Maer and of Malgā were destroyed.” An almost identical form of the same date occurs on Brit. Mus., No. 33,227 (B. 70), which reads <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E</span> [case <span class="small-caps">INIM ANA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">EN-LIL-LA-TA</span>] <span class="small-caps">BAD MA-ER</span>(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) <span class="small-caps">U BAD MA-AL-KA-A BA-AN-GUL-LA</span> ; a similar formula also occurs on Brit. Mus., No. 33,215 (B. 58). Some tablets are dated by reference to the destruction of Maer only, e.g. V.A.Th. 762 (Meissner, No. 105), <span class="small-caps">MU BAD MA-ER(KI) BA-GUL-LA</span>; and Bu. 88–5–12, 363, <span class="small-caps">MU BAD MA-ER(KI)</span>. The tablets Bu. 91–5–9, 2,192, and Bu. 88–5–12, 166, are probably not to be assigned to this year, as their dates seem to refer to the building, or repair, of the fortresses of <span class="small-caps">MAL-GE-A</span> and of <span class="small-caps">MA-ER(KI)</span> respectively.
[^fn-p0313-1]: (47) It is possible that the sign at the beginning of the line is <span class="small-caps">BAD</span>, but the traces appear to be those of two characters. We may conclude that the formula did not refer to the capture or destruction of Isin, in view of its previous subjugation under Sin-muballiṭ; since tablets would not have been dated for thirty years by that event if the city had again revolted in that period and had been again destroyed. Moreover, the era of the taking of Isin must date from the 17th year of Sin-muballiṭ, and not from the 7th year of Ḫammurabi, for a tablet of Rim-Sin is dated in the 27th year after its destruction, and we know that Rim-Sin was defeated and deposed by Ḫammurabi in the 31st year of his reign (see below, p. 236, n. 66).
[^fn-p0313-2]: (48) For the canal named Nuḫuš-niši, see the following note. It is strange that the bank of this canal should be mentioned before the year in which its construction by Ḫammurabi is recorded, but it is possible that the 8th year was occupied with preliminary work on the canal which was completed in the 9th year, or the king may have merely deepened and improved an old canal.
[^fn-p0314-1]: (49) Reference is made to the digging of canals by Ḫammurabi in the formulæ both for this year and for the 33rd year of his reign. Many contract-tablets are dated <span class="small-caps">MU ID</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi*, “The year of the Ḫammurabi-canal,” e.g. Bu. 88–5–12, 137, 175, 176 ; Bu. 91–5–9, 2,464 ; V.A.Th. 856 (Meissner, No. 106), 1,109 (Meissner, No. 12); Bu. 91–5–9, 712 (<span class="small-caps">MU ID-DA</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi*), etc.; and the tablets so dated may be referred to either of these years. The name of the most important of the canals of Ḫammurabi we know from inscription No. VII (see above, pp. 188 ff.) to have been Nuḫuš-niši, “The abundance of the people,” and this name is also referred to in the formulæ upon dated contracts, e.g., Bu. 91–5–9, 3, <span class="small-caps">MU ID</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi Nu-ḫu-uš-ni-ši*, and Bu. 88–5–12, 147 and 188, <span class="small-caps">MU</span> (<span class="small-caps">ID</span>)*Nu-ḫu-uš-ni-ši*. From the cone-inscriptions of Ḫammurabi we learn that in addition to the Nuḫuš-niši Canal he also dug out a channel from the Euphrates to supply Sippar with water (see above, pp. 177 ff.), and it is not improbable that this canal bore the name of Tišid-Bēl, which is mentioned upon some dated contracts, e.g., Bu. 88–5–12, 211, and Bu. 91–5–9, 2,474, both of which are dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> (<span class="small-caps">ID</span>) *Ti-ši-id-*(<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)*Bēl*, “The year of the Tišid-Bēl Canal.” The Tišid-Bēl Canal is mentioned with the Euphrates in the date upon Bu. 88–5–12, 143, which reads <span class="small-caps">MU</span> (<span class="small-caps">ID</span>)*Ti-ši-i*[*d-*(<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)*Bēl*] *u* (<span class="small-caps">ID</span>)<span class="small-caps">BURANUNU</span> ; and the date upon the “case” of V.A.Th. 842 f. (Meissner, No. 110) gives the still fuller formula <span class="small-caps">MU</span> (<span class="small-caps">ID</span>) *T*[*i-ši-i*]*d-*(<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)*Bēl* (<span class="small-caps">ID</span>)<span class="small-caps">BURANUNU MU-UN-BA-AL-LA</span>, “The year in which the canal Tišid-Bēl was dug out to the Euphrates,” the tablet itself giving the abbreviated formula <span class="small-caps">MU</span> (<span class="small-caps">ID</span>)*Ti-ši-id-*(<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)*Bēl*. There is no evidence as to what years to assign the construction of these two canals, but, in view of the reference to the Nuḫuš-niši Canal in the formula for the 8th year, we may provisionally assign the completion of that canal to the 9th year, and the cutting of the Tišid-Bēl Canal to the 33rd year of Ḫammurabi’s reign, or to one of those years for which the formulæ are partly, or entirely, wanting.
[^fn-p0315-1]: (50) To this year belong the contracts Brit. Mus., No. 92,501 (82–9–18, 220), which is dated <span class="small-caps">MU GU-ZA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)*Ṣar-pa-ni-tum*, and Bu. 91–5–9, 374, which is dated <span class="small-caps">MU GIŠ-GU-ZA</span>, etc.
[^fn-p0315-2]: (51) The restoration of this line is taken from the dates upon Bu. 88–5–12, 227, and Bu. 91–5–9, 334, which read <span class="small-caps">MU GU-ZA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">NINNI KA-DINGIR-RA</span>(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>). V.A.Th. 828 (Meissner, No. 13) is dated <span class="small-caps">MU GU-ZA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">NINNI KA-DINGIR-RA</span>, and the date occurs in more abbreviated forms upon Bu. 88–5–12, 169 and 322 (<span class="small-caps">MU GIŠ-GU-ZA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">NINNI</span>), and Bu. 91–5–9, 773 (<span class="small-caps">MU GU-ZA NINNI</span>).
[^fn-p0315-3]: (52) B reads [ . . . . ] V[II]<span class="small-caps">-A-AN</span> ; the date is found upon Brit. Mus., No. 92,584 (Bu. 88–5–12, 713), and Bu. 91–5–9, 2,178, under the form <span class="small-caps">MU ALAM</span> VII<span class="small-caps">-BI</span> ; cf. also Bu. 91–5–9, 780.
[^fn-p0316-1]: (53) Bu. 88–5–12, 488, and Bu. 91–5–9, 2,341, are to be assigned to this year, their dates reading <span class="small-caps">MU</span> <span class="small-caps">GU-ZA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)*Na-bi-um.*
[^fn-p0316-2]: (54) The date upon Bu. 91–5–9, 364, reads <span class="small-caps">MU</span> <span class="small-caps">UŠ-SA</span> <span class="small-caps">E-IGI-ḪAR-SAG-GA</span>, which may perhaps be regarded as an alternative formula for the 20th year ; the first sign in the formula for the 19th year preserved by B, however, appears to be <span class="small-caps">EN</span>, not <span class="small-caps">E</span>.
[^fn-p0316-3]: (55) The restoration is certain, as part of the sign <span class="small-caps">ZA</span> is preserved by B ; the date also occurs upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,337, under the form <span class="small-caps">MU</span> <span class="small-caps">GU-ZA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">MER</span>.
[^fn-p0316-4]: (56) For *Ba-ṣu*, A gives the variant reading [*B*]*a-zi* ; the restoration of the line is taken from the date upon Brit. Mus., No. 92,590 (Bu. 88–5–12, 746) ; the “case” of this tablet, and Brit. Mus., No. 92,595 (Bu. 88–5–12, 241), are dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> <span class="small-caps">BAD</span> (<span class="small-caps">URU</span>) *Ba-ṣu*(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>).
[^fn-p0316-5]: (57) The reference in this line is possibly to the cutting of a canal by Ḫammurabi (as in the formulæ for the 9th and 33rd years), or perhaps to the making of an image of the king.
[^fn-p0316-6]: (58) The traces of the last character but one in this line on both A and B suggest <span class="small-caps">NUN</span> ; it is therefore possible that the name of Sippar should be restored.
[^fn-p0316-7]: (59) The restoration of the name of Enlil is taken from B, col. I, l. 12. The reckoning of the two tablets appears to differ at this point, B inserting an additional year [<span class="small-caps">MU</span> . . . . <span class="small-caps">ZIMBIR</span>(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>)-<span class="small-caps">RA</span> in l. 11] between the 23rd and 24th years of A. It is possible, however, that ll. 11 and 12 of B form a double line, and that both refer to the 24th year.
[^fn-p0317-1]: (60) The verb <span class="small-caps">BA-RU</span> is omitted by A, and the restoration of the name of Sippar is taken from B. <span class="small-caps">BAD</span> may be restored with some probability at the beginning of the line, as some contracts of the reign of Ḫammurabi are dated by the building of the wall of Sippar, e.g. Bu. 88–5–12, 185, <span class="small-caps">MU PIN BAD</span> Zimbir(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>), “The year of the foundation of the wall of Sippar”; cf. also the date upon Brit. Mus., No. 92,575 (Bu. 88–5–12, 645), <span class="small-caps">MU BAD-GAL</span> Zimbir(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>), “The year of the great wall of Sippar.” The contract Bu. 91–5–9, 1,024, is dated simply <span class="small-caps">MU</span> Zimbir(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>), “The year of Sippar,” and may also refer to this year.
[^fn-p0317-2]: (61) The traces of the character before <span class="small-caps">GAL</span> upon B suggest the character <span class="small-caps">A</span>; if this reading be correct, it is possible that the destruction of Umliaš by flood should be assigned to this year and not to the 38th year of Ḫammurabi (see below, p. 238, note 71).
[^fn-p0318-1]: (62) In A the line ends at <span class="small-caps">MAḪ</span>; perhaps the building of the temple Igi-e-nir-kidur-maḫ should be assigned to this year (see below, p. 241, note 72 (4)).
[^fn-p0318-2]: (63) The restoration of <span class="small-caps">ḪE</span> is taken from B. The date is found upon Bu. 91–5–9, 824, under the form <span class="small-caps">MU E-NAM-ḪE E</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>) <span class="small-caps">MER-RA</span>, “The year of E-namḫe, the temple of Rammān,” and upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,460, under the form <span class="small-caps">MU E-ḪE</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">MER</span>. A tablet found at Abu-ḥabbah is also to be assigned to this year, as it is dated <span class="small-caps">MU E-NAM-ḪE</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">MER</span> (see Scheil, *Recueil de travaux*, xvii, 1895, p. 36). E-namḫe, “the House of Abundance,” was the name of the temple of Rammān at Babylon; cf. Brünnow, *Classified List*, No. 2,168.
[^fn-p0318-3]: (64) The name of the goddess Šala is restored from B. The date occurs upon Bu. 88–5–12, 624, under the same formula as that given in the text.
[^fn-p0318-4]: (65) This year is cited upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,425, under the form <span class="small-caps">MU ṢAB-UMMAN ELAM-MA</span> [case <span class="small-caps">ELAM-MA(KI)</span>], and upon Brit. Mus., No. 92,650 (Bu. 91–5–9, 2,440), under the form <span class="small-caps">MU UMMAN ELAM-MA(KI)</span>; the “case” of the latter tablet inserts *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E</span> after <span class="small-caps">MU</span>.
[^fn-p0318-5]: (66) The dates upon Bu. 88–5–12, 339 (<span class="small-caps">MU MA-DA</span> *Ia-mu-ut-ba-lum*), and upon Bu. 88–5–12, 48 (<span class="small-caps">MU MA-DA</span> *E-mu-ut-ba-lum*), probably refer to this year. A fuller formula for the same year occurs on Brit. Mus., No. 33,221 (B. 64); the date of this tablet reads <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL IT</span>(?)-<span class="small-caps">TI ANA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">EN-LIL-BI-TA ŠIG-NA BA-NI-GIN-NA-A MA-DA</span> *Ia-mu-ut-ba-lum* (case inserts determinative <span class="small-caps">ki) u lugal</span> *Ri-im-*(<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)*Sin* <span class="small-caps">šu-ni ki ne-dug</span> (case <span class="small-caps">ne-in-[dug]</span>), “The year of Ḫammurabi the king in which with the help of Anu and Bēl he established his good fortune, and his hand cast to the earth the land of Iamutbal and Rim-Sin the king”; cf. Jensen, *Keilinschriftliche Bibliothek*, III, pt. 1, p. 126 f. The date upon Brit. Mus., No. 33,210 (B. 53), gives the first part of this formula down to the first verb, which reads <span class="small-caps">ni-gin-na-a.</span>
[^fn-p0319-1]: (67) Perhaps restore the line <span class="small-caps">mu umman [eš-nun-na(ki)]</span>, “The year in which the men of Umliaš (were slain with the sword)”; see below, p. 238 f., n. 71.
[^fn-p0319-2]: (68) For the canals cut by Ḫammurabi, see above, p. 232 f., note 49.
[^fn-p0319-3]: (69) This line is restored from the date upon Brit. Mus., No. 33,222 (B. 65); Bu. 88–5–12, 318, gives the slightly variant form <span class="small-caps">mu ana (dingir)ninni u (dingir)na-na-a.</span> A fuller form of the same date is found upon the “case” of Brit. Mus., No. 33,230 (B. 73), which reads <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">lugal-e ana (dingir)ninni (dingir)na-na-a mu-un-dim-ma</span> (on the tablet the signs <span class="small-caps">un</span> and <span class="small-caps">dim</span> are transposed through a scribal error), “The year of Ḫammurabi the king in which for Anu, Ištar, and Nanā was built ( . . . . ).” The name of the temple which was built, or repaired, during this year in honour of these deities is supplied by the following formula from Brit. Mus., No. 33,219 (B. 62) : <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">lugal-e Ana</span> (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)<span class="small-caps">Ninni</span> (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)<span class="small-caps">Na-na-a-a-e-ne-bi-ta E-tur-kalam-ma mu-un-gi-a-an</span>, “The year of Ḫammurabi the king in which for Anu, Ištar, and Nanā the temple E-tur-kalama was restored.” The date upon V.A.Th. 817 (Meissner, No. 82) gives the first part of the preceding formula ; it reads <span class="small-caps">mu Ana</span> (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)<span class="small-caps">Ninni u</span> (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)<span class="small-caps">Na-na-a-e-</span>[<span class="small-caps">ne</span>]<span class="small-caps">-bi-ta</span>.
[^fn-p0320-1]: (70) Perhaps restore <span class="small-caps">mu bad</span> [<span class="small-caps">gal</span> *Kar-ra-<sup>ilu</sup> Šamaš*], “The year in which the great wall (named) Kāra-Šamaš (was built)” ; see below, p. 239 f., note 72.
[^fn-p0320-2]: (71) This line is restored from Brit. Mus., Nos. 33,232 (B. 75) and 33,234 (B. 77), which are dated <span class="small-caps">mu Eš-nun-na(ki) a-gal-gal-la mu-un-gul</span>, the “case” of No. 33,232 giving the slightly fuller formula <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">lugal Eš-nun-na(ki) a-gal-gal-la mu-un-gul-la</span> ; some slight variants occur in the date upon Bu. 91–5–9, 410, which reads [<span class="small-caps">mu E</span>]<span class="small-caps">š-nun-na a-gal-gal mu-un-gul-la</span>, while the verb is omitted in the date upon V.A.Th. 752 (Meissner, No. 87), <span class="small-caps">mu Eš-nun-na(ki) a-gal-gal-la</span>. A tablet found at Abu-ḥabbah, and now in the Imperial Ottoman Museum, is dated <span class="small-caps">mu Eš nun-na(ki)</span> *me* <span class="small-caps">gal-gal-la</span> (see Scheil, *Recueil de travaux*, xvii, p. 35), but it is possible that the sign read as *me* is a carelessly-written <span class="small-caps">a</span>. The contract-tablet Bu. 91–5–9, 1,155, is dated [<span class="small-caps">mu</span>] <span class="small-caps">umman Eš-nun-</span>[<span class="small-caps">na(ki)</span>], “The year in which the men of Umliaš ( . . . . . ),” and Bu. 91–5–9, 691, is dated <span class="small-caps">mu ṣab Eš-nun-na(ki) giš-ku ba-sig</span>, “The year in which the men of Umliaš were slain with the sword.” It is, therefore, conceivable that the flood mentioned in the previously cited formulæ should be treated as a metaphorical description of the capture of the city by Ḫammurabi ; it appears to me more probable, however, that the destruction of Umliaš by flood is to be taken literally, and the capture of the city to be referred to another occasion ; (see above, p. 237, note 67). The “ case ” of Brit. Mus., No. 33,225 (B. 68), is dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> E<span class="small-caps">Š-NUN-NA(KI)</span> <span class="small-caps">A-GAL-GAL-E</span> <span class="small-caps">MU-UN-GUL-E</span>, but the tablet is dated by quite a different formula, which reads <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL</span> <span class="small-caps">UMMAN</span> *Tu-ru-uk-ku Ka-ak-mu-um*(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) <span class="small-caps">U</span> *Su-bi-e* (<span class="small-caps">KI</span>)-<span class="small-caps">BI-TA</span>, “ The year of Ḫammurabi the king in which the people in Turukku, Kakmum, and Subē ( . . . . ).” These two dates are evidently alternative formulæ for the 38th year, and we may perhaps assign to this year Bu. 88–5–12, 471, dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> <span class="small-caps">UMMAN</span> (?) *Tu-ru-ḳu*, and V.A.Th. 766 (Meissner, No. 70), dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> [ . . . ] <span class="small-caps">MUL</span> *Tu-ru-ḳu*.
[^fn-p0321-1]: (72) The formulæ for several years of Ḫammurabi’s reign are entirely missing, or the traces of characters are so incomplete as to render their restoration from dates upon contract-tablets uncertain. Among the dates upon tablets belonging to Ḫammurabi’s reign which have not been identified, the following may be mentioned :— (1) Bu. 91–5–9, 2,480, is dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> (<span class="small-caps">URU</span>)*Ra-bi-ḳu*(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>), “ The year of the city of Rabiḳu,” a date which occurs without either determinative, under the form <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ra-bi-ḳu*, upon Bu. 91–5–9, 831. This date is probably explained on Bu. 91–5–9, 2,515, by the fuller formula <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ra-bi-ḳu*(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) *<sup>m</sup>**I-bi-ik-<sup>ilu</sup> Rammān* <span class="small-caps">BA-DIB</span>, “ The year in which Ibik-Rammān captured the city of Rabiḳu.” (2) The city of Rabiḳu is also mentioned in the formula for another year upon Bu. 88–5–12, 636, <span class="small-caps">MU</span> <span class="small-caps">BAD GAL</span> *Kār-<sup>ilu</sup> Šamaš*(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) <span class="small-caps">U BAD</span> *Ra-bi-ḳu*(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>); cf. also the date upon V.A.Th. 743 (Meissner, No. 62), where we should probably read *Ra-bi-ḳu*(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) in the second line of the date-formula. That this formula refers to the building, and not to the destruction of the walls of Kār-Šamaš and Rabiḳu, is proved by the dates upon Brit. Mus., No. 33,228 (B. 71), <span class="small-caps">MU BAD GAL</span> *Kar-ra-**<sup>ilu</sup>**Šamaš* <span class="small-caps">MU-UN-RU-A</span>, and Brit. Mus., No. 33,233 (B. 76), <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ḫa-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL BAD GAL</span> *Kar-ra-**<sup>ilu</sup>**Šamaš* <span class="small-caps">MU-UN-RU-A</span>, “The year of Ḫammurabi the king in which the great wall of Kāra-Šamaš was built,” and by the still fuller formula on Brit. Mus., No. 33,212 (B. 55), <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *<sup>ilu</sup>**Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E BAD GAL TIG</span> (<span class="small-caps">ID</span>)<span class="small-caps">IDIGNA SAG-BI ḪAR-SAG-GIM MU-UN-IL</span> *Kar-ra-**<sup>ilu</sup>**Šamaš* <span class="small-caps">MU-BI NE-IN-SA MU-UN-RU-A</span>, “The year in which Ḫammurabi the king built on the bank of the Tigris a great wall, whose summit he made high like unto a mountain, and whose name he called Kāra-Šamaš.” V.A.Th. 1,075 (Meissner, No. 51), gives the abbreviated formula <span class="small-caps">MU BAD GAL</span> *Kār-**<sup>ilu</sup>**Šamaš*(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>), which also occurs, with the end of the line imperfectly preserved, on Bu. 91–5–9, 1,137 ; cf. also the dates upon Brit. Mus., Nos. 33,179 (B. 22) and 33,187 (B. 30), which possibly also refer to this year. In the majority of the formulæ cited the wall of the city of Rabiḳu is not mentioned ; it is clear, therefore, that the construction of the wall of Kār-Šamaš was regarded as the more important event of the two. (3) Another date-formula not uncommonly met with is <span class="small-caps">MU</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)*Taš-me-tum*, “The year of Tašmetum,” which occurs upon Bu. 88–5–12, 39, Bu. 91–5–9, 686, and Brit. Mus., No. 33,211 (B. 54). This date is given more fully upon Brit. Mus., No. 33,214 (B. 57), and upon the “cases” of Brit. Mus., Nos. 33,217 (B. 60), 33,220 (B. 63), 33,223 (B. 66), 33,229 (B. 72), and 33,323 (B. 166), which read <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ḫa-am-mu*[*-um*]*-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL</span>[-<span class="small-caps">E</span>] (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)*Taš-me-tum* <span class="small-caps">INIM-ŠAG-ŠAG-</span> <span class="small-caps">GA</span>[-A]-<span class="small-caps">NI</span>, “The year of Ḫammurabi the king in which the goddess Tašmetum made favourable her word.” The same formula, with the omission of Ḫammurabi’s name, occurs also on Bu. 91–5–9, 2,465, and on the “case” of Brit. Mus., No. 33,224 (B. 67). (4) Other contracts of Ḫammurabi’s reign, e.g., Brit. Mus., Nos. 33,213 (B. 56), 33,218 (B. 61), 33,226 (B. 69), and 33,231 (B. 74), are dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL</span>[-E] E-<span class="small-caps">ME-TE-UR-SAG MU-UN-GI-A</span>, “The year of Ḫammurabi the king in which the temple E-me-te-ur-sag was restored.” From the dates upon the “cases” of Brit. Mus., Nos. 33,209 (B. 52) and 33,218 (B. 61) we learn that the temple Igi-e-nir-kidur-maḫ was built in the same year; the formula reads <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Ḫa-am-mu-ra-bi* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E</span> E-<span class="small-caps">ME-TE-UR-SAG-GA</span> [B. 61 omits <span class="small-caps">GA</span>] <span class="small-caps">MU-UN-GI-LAL</span> [B. 61 <span class="small-caps">MU-UN-GI-A</span>] I<span class="small-caps">GI-E-NIR-KI-DUR-MAḪ</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)Z<span class="small-caps">A-MA-MA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)N<span class="small-caps">INNI</span> [B. 61 adds -<span class="small-caps">GE</span>] <span class="small-caps">SAG-BI AN-GIM IL-LA MU-UN-RU-A</span>, “The year of Ḫammurabi the king in which the temple E-me-te-ur-sag was restored, and the temple Igi-e-nir-kidur-maḫ was built for Zamama and Ninni (Ištar), and its summit made high like the heavens.” The tablet of No. 33,209 gives the slightly abbreviated formula <span class="small-caps">MU</span> E-<span class="small-caps">ME-TE-UR-SAG-GA MU-UN-GI-LAL</span> E-<span class="small-caps">IGI</span>(*sic*)-<span class="small-caps">NIR-KI-DUR-MAḪ</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)Z<span class="small-caps">A-MA-MA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)N<span class="small-caps">INNI</span>. The “case”-fragment Brit. Mus., No. 33,278 (B. 121) is also dated in this year.
[^fn-p0323-1]: (73) It is possible that this line should be restored from the date upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,369, which reads <span class="small-caps">MU IŠI</span> Z<span class="small-caps">IMBIR</span>(<span class="small-caps">KI</span>) <span class="small-caps">URU UL</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)U<span class="small-caps">TU-GE</span>.
[^fn-p0323-2]: (74) The List of Kings assigns fifty-five years to the reign of Ḫammurabi; B agrees with A.
[^fn-p0323-3]: (75) Among tablets dated by the formula <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL</span>[-E], may be mentioned Bu. 88–5–12, 182, Bu. 91–5–9, 846, 938, 2,179, and 2,519.
[^fn-p0324-1]: (76) This date occurs upon Bu. 88–5–12, 37, under the form <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal dagal ar-gi i-ni-gar-ra</span> ; upon Bu. 91–5–9, 511, <span class="small-caps">mu dagal ar-gi ki-en-gi</span>(<span class="small-caps">ki</span>) ; and upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,444, <span class="small-caps">mu dagal ar-ge</span>, the “case” of the last-named tablet giving the fuller formula <span class="small-caps">mu dagal ar-ge ki-en-gi</span>(<span class="small-caps">ki</span>) <span class="small-caps">urdu</span>. It is probable that V.A.Th. 1,246 (Meissner, No. 33) should also be assigned to this year.
[^fn-p0324-2]: (77) The scribe of A has omitted the second *na* by mistake. A fuller form of the name of this canal is given on Bu. 91–5–9, 2,458, in the date-formula <span class="small-caps">mu id</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na Na-ga-ab-nu-ḫu-uš-ni-ši* <span class="small-caps">mu-ba-al</span>, “The year in which the canal of Samsu-iluna (named) Nagab-nuḫuš-niši was dug.” The date occurs also upon Brit. Mus., Nos. 33,246 (B. 92) and 33,256 (B. 99) under the form <span class="small-caps">mu</span> [*Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal</span>] <span class="small-caps">id</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na Na-ga-ab-nu-uḫ-ši* <span class="small-caps">mu-un-ba-la</span>, and upon Bu. 88–5–12, 332, and V.A.Th. 787 (Meissner, No. 59) under the form <span class="small-caps">mu id</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* [<span class="small-caps">lugal-e</span>] *Na-ga-ab-nu-uḫ-ši* ; the date upon Bu. 91–5–9, 2,485, is partly covered by fragments of the “case,” but gives the same formula. It is probably the third year of Samsu-iluna, which is referred to on Bu. 88–5–12, 617, under the alternative formula <span class="small-caps">mu uš-sa uš</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-*[*na*].
[^fn-p0324-3]: (78) This date occurs on Brit. Mus., Nos. 33,237 (B. 80), 33,238 (B. 81), 33,242 (B. 85), 33,243 (B. 86), and 33,252 (B. 95), under the form <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal</span>[-<span class="small-caps">e</span>] <span class="small-caps">id</span>[-<span class="small-caps">da</span>] *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">ḪE-GAL MU-UN-BA</span>[-<span class="small-caps">AL</span>]-<span class="small-caps">LA</span>. Brit. Mus., No. 33,259 (B. 102), is dated <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-n*[*a* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E</span>] (<span class="small-caps">ID</span>)<span class="small-caps">ḪE-GAL</span> [ . . . . . ]; upon Brit. Mus., No. 33,245 (B. 88), the canal is termed simply “the Samsu-iluna-Canal” in the date-formula <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E ID</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">MU-UN-BA-AL-LA</span>, the “case” reading <span class="small-caps">ID</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">ḪE-GAL</span>; while the abbreviated formula given in the text is found upon V.A.Th. 841 (Meissner, No. 83), l. 3.
[^fn-p0325-1]: (79) Bu. 91–5–9, 272 and 439, are dated by the formula for this year as given in the text; the date upon V.A.Th. 841 (Meissner, No. 83) reads <span class="small-caps">MU GU-ZA BARA-GE</span>; and Brit. Mus., No. 33,235 (B. 78) gives the fuller formula <span class="small-caps">MU GIŠ-GU-ZA BARA-GE MU-UN-NA-DIM-MA</span>. The dates upon Brit. Mus., No. 33,257 (B. 100), V.A.Th. 643 (Meissner, No. 55), and I.O.M., No. 183 (see Scheil, *Recueil*, xix, p. 57), probably also refer to this year.
[^fn-p0325-2]: (80) Cf. Brünnow, *Classified List*, No. 10,623. To this year belong Brit. Mus., Nos. 33,161 (B. 4), 33,240 (B. 83), and 33,269 (B. 112); in its fuller form the date refers to the restoration of the temples E-me-te-ur-sag and E-sagil.
[^fn-p0325-3]: (81) This early reference to an invasion of Babylonia by the Kassites during the period of the First Dynasty is of great interest. The contract Bu. 88–5–12, 114, is dated in this year under the same abbreviated formula <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal-e umman</span> *Ka-aš-šu-u*, “The year of Samsu-iluna the king in which the army of the Kassites ( . . . . ).”
[^fn-p0326-1]: (82) Brit. Mus., No. 33,248, Bu. 88–5–12, 715, and Bu. 91–5–9, 396, are dated in this year; in the formula upon the two last-mentioned tablets <span class="small-caps">umman</span> is expressed by the sign <span class="small-caps">ṣab</span>.
[^fn-p0326-2]: (83) From the abbreviated formula given in the text it is not clear whether the walls of Ur and Erech were built or destroyed in this year; that the former was the case may perhaps be assumed from the date upon a contract in the Imperial Ottoman Museum, No. 642, which reads <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *<sup>ilu</sup>* *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">bad uru(ki) u unug(ki) mu-un-na-dim</span>, “The year of Samsu-iluna in which the wall(s) of Ur and Erech were built” (see Scheil, *Recueil*, xix, p. 60).
[^fn-p0326-3]: (84) The tablet Brit. Mus., No. 92,663 (Bu. 91–5–9, 2,518), is dated in this year according to the formula in the text; its “case” gives the fuller formula <span class="small-caps">mu kur am-si-a an-ga-a</span>.
[^fn-p0326-4]: (85) Bu. 88–5–12, 194, which is dated <span class="small-caps">mu lugal im-</span>[ . . . ], may probably be assigned to this year.
[^fn-p0327-1]: (86) B gives the variant reading <span class="small-caps">i</span>(i.e. <span class="small-caps">ni</span>)-<span class="small-caps">si-in</span>[ . . . . ]. For a similar formula employed during the reign of Sin-muballiṭ, see above, p. 228 f., and cf. note 39. It may be noted that some contracts of Samsu-iluna's reign are dated by the restoration of the wall of Isin, e.g. Bu. 88–5–12, 153 and 707, which give the formula <span class="small-caps">mu</span> [*Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal</span>] <span class="small-caps">bad i-si-in</span>[<span class="small-caps">-na</span>](<span class="small-caps">ki</span>) <span class="small-caps">ki-bi-ku ne-in-ge-a</span>, “The year of Samsu - iluna the king in which the wall of Isin was restored to its place.”
[^fn-p0327-2]: (87) The restoration is taken from the formulæ upon Bu. 88–5–12, 225 and 706, both of which are dated in this year, and from which it would appear that the wall in question was built at Sippar.
[^fn-p0327-3]: (88) B. reads <span class="small-caps">mu e-babbar e</span>[(<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>) <span class="small-caps">Utu</span>].
[^fn-p0327-4]: (89) It is possible that Bu. 91–5–9, 545 and 1,016, are dated in this year.
[^fn-p0328-1]: (90) Bu. 88–5–12, 685, 687, and 699, are all dated in this year by the formula given in the text.
[^fn-p0328-2]: (91) The date upon the tablet No. 174 in the Ottoman Museum (see Scheil, *Recueil*, xix, p. 57) possibly gives a variant formula for this year.
[^fn-p0328-3]: (92) The date upon Bu. 91–5–9, 330, which refers to this year reads, <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Sa-am-su-i-lu-na* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL</span>-<span class="small-caps">E</span> <span class="small-caps">UŠ</span>-<span class="small-caps">SA</span> <span class="small-caps">ID</span>-<span class="small-caps">AG</span>-<span class="small-caps">GA</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)E<span class="small-caps">N</span>-<span class="small-caps">LIL</span>-<span class="small-caps">LA</span>; cf. also Bu. 88–5–12, 135.
[^fn-p0328-4]: (93) B agrees with A in assigning thirty-eight years to Samsu-iluna; the List of Kings assigns thirty-five years to his reign. A, which ends with Samsu-iluna’s reign, is dated in the reign of Ammizaduga, but of the second line of the formula only traces of characters remain.
[^fn-p0330-1]: (94) The few signs preserved of Col. III of B, to judge from their position on the tablet, probably refer to the early years of the reign of Abēšu’.
[^fn-p0330-2]: (95) What remains of Col. IV of B refers to the early years of Ammiditana’s reign.
[^fn-p0330-3]: (96) The formula for this year should possibly be restored from the dates upon Bu. 91–5–9, 355, 397, and 419, which read <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Am-mi-di-ta-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal-e ad-gi-a gu-la (dingir)</span>U<span class="small-caps">tu (dingir)</span>*Marduk*-<span class="small-caps">bi-da(-ge)</span>, “The year of Ammiditana the king in which through the mighty counsel of Šamaš and Marduk ( . . . . ).” This was perhaps the first year of Ammiditana’s reign.
[^fn-p0330-4]: (97) The line has been restored from the date upon Bu. 88–5–12, 281, which reads <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Am-mi-di-ta-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal-e siba im-tuk še-ga (dingir)</span>U<span class="small-caps">tu</span>, and that upon Bu. 88–5–12, 271, <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Am-mi-di-t*[*a-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal-e</span>] <span class="small-caps">siba im-tuk še-g</span>[<span class="small-caps">a (dingir)</span>U<span class="small-caps">tu</span>] <span class="small-caps">bi-da-</span>[<span class="small-caps">ge</span>]. The date occurs upon Bu. 88–5–12, 197, in the abbreviated form <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Am-mi-te-ta-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal siba im-tuk še-ga</span>.
[^fn-p0331-1]: (98) These two lines have been restored from V.A.Th. 799 (Meissner, No. 19), which is dated <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Am-mi-di-ta-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal-e eš-bar-ta dingir-gal-gal-la sag-du-a-ni an-la-al nam-a-gal</span> (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)*Marduk*-<span class="small-caps">ge</span>(?), “The year of Ammiditana the king in which by the decision of the great gods he raised (?) his head and the might of Marduk ( . . . . )”; the date occurs on Bu. 91-5-9, 393, in the abbreviated form <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Am-mi-di-ta-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal eš-bar-maḫ-a dingir-gal-gal</span>, “The year of Ammiditana the king in which through the exalted decisions of the great gods ( . . . . ).” It is possible that we may see a variant of this formula in the date upon Bu. 88-5-12, 218, which reads <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Am-mi-di-ta-na* <span class="small-caps">lugal-e nam-a-gal-la</span> (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)*Marduk*-<span class="small-caps">ge</span>(?) <span class="small-caps">in-ne-en-gar-ra-ta</span>(?), “The year of Ammiditana the king in which the might of Marduk was established”; cf. also the dates upon Bu. 88-5-12, 263 and 330, and Bu. 91-5-9, 357 and 363.
[^fn-p0332-1]: (99) The making of images is also referred to in the formulæ for the 23rd, 26th, 28th, and 30th years, and several contracts of Ammiditana’s reign are also dated by similar events ; but few of the formulæ on the contracts correspond to the traces of characters in the text.
[^fn-p0332-2]: (100) The reading <span class="small-caps">E</span> is not certain.
[^fn-p0332-3]: (101) The tablets Bu. 91–5–9, 496 and 734, are probably to be assigned to this year.
[^fn-p0332-4]: (102) The date upon Bu. 88–5–12, 522, refers to this year ; it reads <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Am-mi-di-ta-na* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E</span> (<span class="small-caps">DINGIR</span>)<span class="small-caps">URU UR-SAG-GAL-</span>[ . . . ].
[^fn-p0332-5]: (103) Bu. 91–5–9, 736, 781, and 852, are dated in this year.
[^fn-p0332-6]: (104) This formula is found with slight variants in the date upon Bu. 91–5–9, 369, which reads <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Am-mi-di-ta-na* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E ALAM NAM-NUN-NA-NI</span> ; from the fuller formula upon Bu. 88–5–12, 10, it may be inferred that an image of Samsu-iluna was also set up in this year. The date upon Bu. 91-5-9, 471, reads <span class="small-caps">MU</span> *Am-mi-di-ta-na* <span class="small-caps">LUGAL-E ALAM NAM-LUGAL-LA-NA ALAM GUŠKIN-</span>[ . . . ]<span class="small-caps">-GA ŠU-NE-IN-GAB-A</span>, and is perhaps a variant form of this date ; cf. also the shorter formula on Bu. 88-5-12, 635.
[^fn-p0333-1]: (105) If the reading of the god's name as Ninib is correct, the tablets Bu. 91-5-9, 359 and 895, are to be assigned to this year.
[^fn-p0333-2]: (106) Some tablets of Ammiditana's reign are dated by the building of the wall of Iškun(sometimes written Iskun)-Marduk (cf. Bu. 88-5-12, 49, 302, and 305, and Bu. 91-5-9, 764), and the suggested restoration of the text is taken from them.
[^fn-p0334-1]: (107) The tablets Bu. 88–5–12, 435 and 864, and Bu. 91–5–9, 1,203, are dated in this year; cf. also the dates upon Bu. 91–5–9, 352 and 608.
[^fn-p0334-2]: (108) The List of Kings assigns twenty-five years to the reign of Ammiditana.
[^fn-p0334-3]: (109) The restoration of this line is taken from the dates upon Bu. 88–5–12, 158, 215, and 283, and Bu. 91–5–9, 753, which read <span class="small-caps">mu</span> *Am-mi-za-du-ga* <span class="small-caps">lugal-e siba-zi še-ga</span> (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)<span class="small-caps">Utu</span> (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)<span class="small-caps">Marduk-bi-da</span>[<span class="small-caps">-ge</span>].
[^fn-p0335-1]: (110) B was clearly compiled in the tenth year of Ammizaduga’s reign. The List of Kings assigns him twenty-two years.
[^fn-p0335-2]: (111) This estimate agrees with A ; see above, p. 240 f.
[^fn-p0335-3]: (112) A also assigns thirty-eight years to Samsu-iluna ; see above, p. 246 f.
[^fn-p0335-4]: (113) The List of Kings assigns twenty-five years to Abēšu’ ; B’s reading should perhaps be restored as [twenty]-eight.
[^fn-p0335-5]: (114) The restoration of the number is taken from B, Col. V, l. 18 ; see the preceding page.

## APPENDIX

### APPENDIX.

I. <span class="small-caps">Inscription of Marduk-šāpik-zērim, King of Babylon about B.C. 1100</span> (Brit. Mus., No. 26,295).—This inscription, the text of which was included in Vol. I (see pl. 133, No. 70), is a copy[^fn-p0336-1] on clay of an older inscription made in the fifteenth year of Kandalanu, <span class="small-caps">B.C.</span> 633. The following is a transliteration of the text :—

#### <span class="small-caps">Transliteration.</span>

> <span class="small-caps">Obv.</span> [ . . . . . . . . ] - <span class="small-caps">a</span>  
> [ . . . . . ] - <span class="small-caps">gu</span> - <span class="small-caps">e</span>  
> [ . . . ] <span class="small-caps">dingir</span> - <span class="small-caps">e</span> - <span class="small-caps">ne</span> - <span class="small-caps">ge</span>  
> [...] (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)<span class="small-caps">Lugal lugal-he-ir-an-ki-a</span>  
> 5 <span class="small-caps">ur</span> - <span class="small-caps">maḫ</span> <span class="small-caps">e</span> - [<span class="small-caps">sag</span>] <span class="small-caps">il</span> - <span class="small-caps">la</span> 5  
> <span class="small-caps">e</span> - <span class="small-caps">zi</span> - <span class="small-caps">da</span> - <span class="small-caps">bi</span>  
> <span class="small-caps">u</span> <span class="small-caps">mu</span> - <span class="small-caps">un</span> <span class="small-caps">Bar</span> - <span class="small-caps">zi</span> - <span class="small-caps">pa(ki)</span>  
> <span class="small-caps">ḫi</span> - <span class="small-caps">gal</span> <span class="small-caps">e</span> - <span class="small-caps">zi</span> - <span class="small-caps">da</span>  
> <span class="small-caps">lugal</span> - <span class="small-caps">a</span> - <span class="small-caps">ni</span> - <span class="small-caps">ir</span>  
> 10 (<span class="small-caps">dingir</span>)*Marduk* - *ša* *bi* *ik* - 10  
> *zi* - *ri* *im*  
> <span class="small-caps">me</span> - <span class="small-caps">a</span> <span class="small-caps">tig</span> <span class="small-caps">gu</span> - <span class="small-caps">de</span> - <span class="small-caps">a</span> - <span class="small-caps">ni</span>  
> <span class="small-caps">nun</span> - <span class="small-caps">šu</span> <span class="small-caps">du</span> <span class="small-caps">du</span> - <span class="small-caps">a</span> <span class="small-caps">ni</span>  
> <span class="small-caps">ki</span> - <span class="small-caps">gar</span> <span class="small-caps">e</span> <span class="small-caps">zi</span> - <span class="small-caps">da</span>  
> <span class="small-caps">Rev.</span> <span class="small-caps">sun</span> - <span class="small-caps">bi</span> <span class="small-caps">ni</span> - <span class="small-caps">diri</span> - <span class="small-caps">ga</span> 15  
> <span class="small-caps">ši</span> - <span class="small-caps">in</span> - <span class="small-caps">gi</span> - <span class="small-caps">ku</span> - <span class="small-caps">dim</span> <span class="small-caps">ma</span>  
> <span class="small-caps">ki</span> - <span class="small-caps">bi</span> - <span class="small-caps">ku</span>  
> <span class="small-caps">mu</span> - <span class="small-caps">un</span> <span class="small-caps">na</span> - <span class="small-caps">gi</span>  
> *ki* - *ma* *la* - *bi* - *ri* - *nu* 20 *ša - ṭi   ir - ma   ba - ar - im* 20  
> <span class="small-caps">IM   GID - DA</span>   *<sup>m ilu</sup>* *Nabū - šum - līšir*  
> *bu   kur*   *<sup>m</sup>* *Ḫu - za - bi*  
> *<sup>arḫu</sup>* *Nisannu*   *šattu*   15*<sup>KAM</sup>*  
> *Kan - da - la   nu*   *šarri*  
> 25 *Bābili*   *<sup>KI</sup>* 25

##### <span class="small-caps">Translation.</span>

[For Nabū . . . . the messenger] of the gods, [the . . . . ] of Marduk[^fn-p0337-1] the king . . . . of heaven and earth, the lion of E-sagila and E-zida, his king, hath Marduk-šāpik-zērim, . . . . his minister, . . . . , increased the structure of E-zida in its old age, and hath built it anew, and hath set it up in its place.

Written and revised according to its original. The tablet[^fn-p0337-2] of Nabū-šum-līšir, the first-born son of Ḫuzabi. The month Nisan, the fifteenth year of Kandalanu, king of Babylon.

<span class="small-caps">II. Plan of Bīt-Sippar-iaḫrurum, made during the period of the First Dynasty of Babylon</span> (Brit. Mus., No. 86,394).—A copy of this plan is published as an Appendix to the texts in Vol. II (see pl. 242, No. 107). The plan is sketched upon the obverse of the tablet, while the reverse gives the following description and date : *u-zu-ur-ti Bīt-Sippar<sup>KI</sup>-ia-aḫ-ru-rum. <sup>arḫu</sup>Simānu ūmu* 1*<sup>KAM</sup>* <span class="small-caps">MU UŠ-SA . . . . SIBA KI-AG (DINGIR)EN-LIL-LA</span>, “ Plan[^fn-p0337-3] of Bīt[^fn-p0338-1]-Sippar-iaḫrurum. The first day of the month Sivan, the year after that in which . . . . the beloved shepherd of Bēl ( . . . . . ).” The sketch on the obverse represents the ground-plan of a building with eleven principal chambers ; the area occupied by the rooms is in the form of a rough square, with one corner missing. The building faces the <span class="small-caps">SILA-DAGAL</span> (Sem. *sūḳu rapšu*), “the main street,” which is marked along the side parallel to the top of the tablet. Looking towards the street we see that the three chambers on the extreme right probably did not form part of the original building, inasmuch as they break the symmetry of the plan. The building consisted of two long, narrow chambers, each divided by a partition ; they are flanked by three chambers on each side, the three additional chambers on the right having possibly been added at a later period. Twelve doorways are marked in the walls, giving access from room to room ; it is noticeable that no doorways are marked in the walls of two small chambers in the front of the building on the right. The innermost portion of the central chamber farthest from the street is labelled *e-ši-ir-tum*, which may perhaps be rendered “shrine,” while two of the chambers on the extreme right and left are labelled <span class="small-caps">PA-LUL</span>, a phrase which also occurs on a plan published by Thureau-Dangin (*Revue d’assyriologie*, vol. iv, p. 23), and which he translates as “(chamber of) the chief musician.”[^fn-p0338-2] The Bīt-Sippar-iaḫrurum may well have been a building under the control of the priests of the Temple of the Sun-god in Sippar, and some of its chambers may have been employed for storing the tithes and taxes collected in Sippar-iaḫrurum.[^fn-p0338-3]

### Notes

[^fn-p0336-1]: (1) Though the text was doubtless taken from an inscription of Marduk-šāpik-zērim, the copy is not a facsimile of the inscription, for many of the characters are archaistic, and not genuinely archaic, in form.
[^fn-p0337-1]: (2) For <span class="small-caps">Lugal</span>, or *Šarru*, as a title of the god Marduk, see Jensen, *Kosmologie*, p. 145.
[^fn-p0337-2]: (3) The ideogram <span class="small-caps">IM-GID-DA</span> occurs with this meaning in contracts of the Neo-Babylonian period.
[^fn-p0337-3]: (4) That the meaning “plan” is to be assigned to *u-zu-ur-ti* (= *uṣurti*) is clear. The word is probably to be connected with *uṣurtu*, “enclosure,” rather than with *uṣurtu*, “sculpture, bas-relief.”
[^fn-p0338-1]: (5) It is preferable to take *Bīt* as part of the title of the building, rather than to render the phrase as *bīt Sippar-iaḫrurum*, “a house of Sippar-iaḫrurum.”
[^fn-p0338-2]: (6) The Semitic form of the title would be *akil zammeri* ; this rendering, however, is far from certain.
[^fn-p0338-3]: (7) For Sippar-iaḫrurum, see above, p. 118, note 2.

#### מ

ma, copula, “and,” attached to verbs.

ma, enclitic particle ; employed before direct speech, (1) in letter-formula attached to *ḳibi* and to the proper name after *umma*, (2) attached to personal pronoun, or substantive, after *umma* introducing a quotation ; attached to a word for emphasis : **1** (p. 103), 21, **5** (p. 16), 10, **8** (p. 65), Rev. 6, **14** (p. 12), 6, 10, **27** (p. 83), Rev. 5, **38** (p. 108), Rev. 3, 9, **72** (p. 52), 22, **76** (p 28), 11, **77** (p. 85), 9 ; gives indefiniteness to a word : *ša-ni-a-am-ma* **1** (p. 103), 22 ; *ša-nu-tim-ma* **43** (p. 105), 29.

### V.<br />GENERAL INDEX.

[N.B.—The references are to the pages of Vol. III unless otherwise stated.]

**Abēšu'**, king of Babylon, writer of thirteen letters, 130–156; father of Ammiditana, 207 f. ; remains of date-formulæ for early years, 248 f. ; length of reign, 253, n. 113.

**Abiatum**, son of Ili-gimlanni, of Kappanu, a captain, 114 ff.

—— son of Iakrēm, 87 f.

**Abum-waga[ . . . . ]**, shepherd, 70, 72.

**Ammiditana**, king of Babylon, writer of two letters, 157–161 ; king of Martu, 207 f.; buildings of, lxii f.; his images made in the 23rd, 26th, 28th, and 30th years of his reign, 250 f. ; date-formulæ for reign, 248–253 ; length of reign, 252, n. 108.

**Ammiditana-canal**, cut in 22nd year of Ammiditana, 250 f.

**Amur-dannusu**, shepherd, 70, 72.

**Ana**, Sumerian name of Anu (q.v.).

**Ana-minišu-emid**, mentioned, 112 f.

**Ani-ellati**, a *ragānu*, 23 ff.

**Anu**, god, 180, 182, 184 f., 186 f., 188, 190, 199, 205 ; E-tur-kalama restored in his honour in 34th year of Ḫammurabi, 236 ff. ; bride of, 195 f.

**Aphek**, capture of the ark at, Vol. I, p. xlii.

—— shepherd, 70, 72.

—— shepherd, of the city of Ili-idinnam, 71, 73.

—— shepherd, 71, 74.

—— son of Simti[ . . . ]ni, 87 f.

**Arad-Nabium**, chief shepherd, 71, 74.

**Ašur-narara**, king of Assyria, recipient of letter from Rammān-šum-nāṣir, Vol. I, p. xxiii, n. 2.

**Babylon**, its great wall built in the 5th year of Sumula-ilu, 214 f. ; its wall rebuilt in 2nd year of Apil-Sin, 222 f. ; temple of Ištar rebuilt there by Apil-Sin, 222 f., n. 26 ; throne of Šamaš set up there by Apil-Sin, 224 f. ; its prosperity during Ḫammurabi's reign, 178 f. ; granary built there by Ḫammurabi, 192 f. ; temple of Rammān built there by Ḫammurabi, 236 f. ; thrones of Nannar and Ištar set up there by Ḫammurabi, 230 f., 232 f. ; arrangements for transport of Elamite goddesses to, 6 ff. ; summons of a ship to, 155 f. ; summons of ship-captains to, 60 f. ; summons of a priest to, 147 f. ; summons of temple-officials to, 75 f. ; summons of two officials to, 90 f. ; summons of a man to, 92 ; order to

bring sheep to, 162 f., 164, 165 ; summons to, during seed-time, 128 f. ; sending of dates and sesame-seed to, 54 f. ; sending of monies to, 48 ; sending of wood to, 52 f. ; despatch of messengers from, 137 f. ; sending of a case thither for trial, 42 ff. ; order for trial of a case at, 135 f. ; return of recaptured slave to, 133 f. ; sending of tribute to, 12 f. ; bringing of tribute to, 124 f., 141 f. ; bringing of silver as revenue to, 153 f. ; tribute due to, 143 f., 160 f. ; summons of revenue-collectors to, 45 ff. ; king of (title), in historical inscriptions, passim ; men of, 157 ff.

**Baker**, expression for, 43 f., n. 5 ; attached to the various temples, 44, n. 5 ; summons of a b. before Ḫammurabi, 93 ; restoration of a b. to his former position, 103 f. ; report on four bakers, 103 f. ; scribe of the temple-bakers, 42 ff. ; scribe of the bakers of Emutbalum, 103 f.

—— baker, of Emutbalum, 103 f.

—— female slave of, 133 f.

**Ḫammu**, god, lxvi, n. 4.

**Ḫammurabi**, king of Babylon, variant forms of name, lxvi, n. 4; meaning of name, lxv f., n. 4; writer of fifty-five letters to Sin-idinnam, 3–116; general character of his letters, xxix; his portrait from his letters, xx; inscriptions of, 172–194; inscriptions referring to, 195–198; campaigns of, lxvii f.; buildings of, lxi f.; canals cut by, lxiv, 232 f., n. 49; reforms undertaken by, 229 f., n. 44; brought prosperity to Sippar and Babylon, 178 f.; king of Martu, 195 f.; his title *adda*, 198; fragment of statue of, 197; sculpture portrait of, lxiii, n. 3, 196, n. 5, and frontispiece; votive inscription in his honour, 195 f.; hymn celebrating the greatness of his reign, 172 ff.; date-formulæ for reign, 228–241; length of reign, 241, n. 74.
